# Services — Master delivery checklist

**Source of truth:** this file tracks the entire Services platform (backend, frontend, admin, docs).
**Status:** living acceptance register; statuses are evidence-based, not a claim that CI is green.
**Baseline:** 2026-11-15. Previous 85% estimate is historical and **not verified**; do not treat it as a calculated completion percentage.

## Rules

- `[x]` = verified complete: attach commit/PR **and** passing relevant CI or reproducible acceptance evidence.
- `[ ]` = not yet verified. `Prepared` means work was implemented/reported but has not met the verification gate; `Pending` means no completion evidence is established.
- Each change must update this file in the **same PR/commit** as the task or immediately after evidence is available.
- Record evidence under `## Evidence log`, including repository, SHA, CI URL, date and result. Never mark green based on empty GitHub commit-status responses.
- Do not compute a single readiness percentage from unverified checkboxes. Once verified, calculate `verified completed / total acceptance items * 100` (equal weights unless a separately approved weighted scheme is committed).
- Any regression reopens the corresponding checkbox; changes in scope require updating the denominator.
- Release gate: all mandatory acceptance items verified, all main CI green, E2E passing, security review and staging sign-off.

### 2026-11-21 — OTP gateway raw-header integrity boundary (PR CI pending)
- Backend branch `test/otp-gateway-raw-header-boundary-20261121`: added a **reference-only** raw header-pair verifier that rejects duplicate authorization, idempotency, signature and Content-Type fields *before* `Headers` coalesces them; rejects transfer/content encoding and malformed/oversized header entries. Added real HTTPS-adapter roundtrip and negative regression tests.
- **Not accepted yet:** CI result, merge and staging sign-off pending. The helper is NOT a deployed listener, and the actual gateway must still validate HTTP method/route, bound request streaming bytes, real raw-header provenance, TLS/mTLS, approved KZ residency and vendor delivery. No checkbox/readiness change.

### 2026-11-21 — OTP gateway raw HTTP envelope verification (PR pending)

Regression tests use the real HTTPS adapter and reject ambiguous paths, lengths, invalid UTF-8 and oversized bodies.

### 2026-11-21 — OTP gateway bounded streaming body reference (verification pending)
- Backend branch `test/otp-gateway-bounded-stream-20261121`: introduced an **unwired**, fail-closed AsyncIterable<Buffer> stream accumulator capped at 1024 bytes, rejecting oversize chunks before retaining them, non-buffer inputs, empty bodies and aborted reads. Tests cover split-byte delivery, exact limit, overflow cancellation, and abort.
- Not a deployed HTTP listener, and does not guarantee live request cancellation while awaiting a stalled stream: **production integration must enforce socket/deadline timeouts**, raw-header provenance, TLS/mTLS, HTTP envelope verification, KZ residency and provider sign-off. PR CI/merge/staging pending; no acceptance/readiness increase.

### 2026-11-21 — stalled OTP request stream abort handling (PR CI pending)
- Backend branch `test/otp-gateway-stalled-stream-abort-20261121`: reference-only bounded stream reader now races awaiting `next()` against caller AbortSignal and promptly fails closed if a source stalls; best-effort iterator cleanup does not hold abort rejection waiting for a stuck `return()`. Regression tests cover stalled `next()`, abort during iteration, and nonblocking cleanup.
- The **real HTTP transport must still destroy/cancel the socket** on timeout and enforce network deadlines. This is not a deployed listener, provider integration or staging acceptance. PR-head CI/merge pending; do not mark an acceptance item complete or increase readiness percentage.

### 2026-11-21 — OTP stream cleanup fail-closed hardening (PR CI pending)

Tests cover oversized chunks with indefinitely pending iterator cleanup, malformed chunks, synchronous cleanup failures and rejected cleanup promises, ensuring generic sanitized failure.

### 2026-11-21 — OTP gateway composed ingress self-deadline (PR CI pending)

### 2026-11-22 — reference OTP mTLS HTTP response dispatch (PR CI pending)
- Backend branch `feat/otp-mtls-http-dispatch-handler-20261122`: added an **unwired** HTTP callback for the existing trusted mTLS listener factory. It uses the bounded raw-header/OTP HMAC verification and durable ledger dispatcher, returns only the canonical `202 {accepted:true,providerRef}` for accepted deliveries, `409 {accepted:false}` for in-flight/conflicting idempotency, and generic `503 {accepted:false}` for validation/infrastructure failures; unknown path is `404` before body processing. Responses include `no-store`, exact body byte count and `nosniff`. Tests cover accepted receipt, conflict/in-flight no vendor send, invalid payload, wrong route and invalid deadlines. **The handler must never be registered on an unauthenticated route; only pass it into the mTLS server factory.**
- No provider, listener interface binding, KZ residency/network-perimeter enforcement, operational CA/certificate controls or real staging acceptance has been deployed. CI/merge and acceptance pending; no readiness inflation.

### 2026-11-22 — reject future-dated OTP claims from age histogram (CI verified reference)
- Branch `fix/otp-reservation-future-timestamp-detection-20261122` narrows the recent RESERVED bucket to only timestamps not later than PostgreSQL `now()`. Future-dated claims are not quietly treated as new: the existing total-vs-bucket integrity check fails closed with a sanitized error. Unit regression verifies the upper time bound, and real isolated PostgreSQL E2E adds a +2-hour reservation under a SELECT-only observer role and checks the histogram fails closed.
- Verified reference-only safety regression: [PR #424](https://github.com/nurgeldiserikbay/services-back/pull/424), exact PR head `e76061afa5f86f947d627cedeb930d907f4cc87e`, [Backend foundation CI #4509 PASS](https://github.com/nurgeldiserikbay/services-back/actions/runs/45049759724), squash merge `07c6390dcf6fae27c4fb398c3d8dcfd647930e3c`. This does not auto-reset/retry OTP, establish alert SLOs, deploy a provider or change Services app Prisma. Production provider/KZ staging/security sign-off remains pending; readiness unchanged.

### 2026-11-22 — OTP histogram exact PostgreSQL boundary regression (CI verified reference)
- Branch `test/otp-histogram-exact-boundaries-20261122`: new isolated PostgreSQL E2E pins all five reservation fixture timestamps to the **same transaction-stable database `now()`**. Confirms exactly 5m belongs to the recent bucket, just older than 5m belongs to the middle bucket, exactly 1h belongs to middle and just older than 1h belongs to the older bucket. Count query executes as a column-scoped, SELECT-only observer role and still denies idempotency-key disclosure.

## Evidence log

### 2026-11-21 — isolated gateway inbound authentication and signed JSON verification (PR CI pending)

- Branch `feat/otp-gateway-verified-ingress-boundary-20261121`: new `otp-gateway-verified-request.reference.ts` validates an **isolated, unwired** gateway ingress request before any provider/ledger call: exact HTTPS adapter JSON serialization/schema, BFF Bearer token checked with `timingSafeEqual`, strict 64-hex HMAC idempotency-key and domain-separated HMAC payload fingerprint checked in constant time, bounded raw payload and validated OTP parameters. Rejects modified or duplicated JSON properties, extra keys, invalid token, altered code/recipient, malformed signatures and ambiguous serialization. The wrapper passes only the parsed, verified payload to a gateway-owned vendor factory, never a client-controlled vendor callback.
- The adapter-to-gateway roundtrip and tamper-negative tests are local reference tests. ### 2026-11-21 — standalone PostgreSQL claim-to-provider OTP gateway reference (CI pending)

- Branch `feat/otp-gateway-ledger-dispatch-reference-20261121`: `OtpGatewayLedgerDispatchReference` orchestrates a **dedicated, unwired gateway** ledger and abstract vendor send port. A durably committed first `send` claim is the only route to provider dispatch; identical `in_flight` duplicates do not send, different fingerprints conflict, and existing `accepted` receipts replay without a second provider call. Only after the vendor returns an accepted reference is PostgreSQL CAS finalization attempted; an unknown vendor outcome or failed database acknowledgement remains RESERVED for reconciliation—**never automatic retry/reclaim**.
- Disposable PostgreSQL-schema tests exercise competing multi-client requests while vendor delivery is deliberately held open, accepted receipt replay, content conflict, and an ambiguous vendor failure that stays permanently unresolved until reviewed. Fail-closed database errors, aborted requests and missing acknowledgement are also covered. This is a **reference-only service**, not connected to the Nest application or a real SMS/email provider. ### 2026-11-21 — isolated PostgreSQL gateway idempotency ledger (PR-head CI pending)

- Branch `feat/otp-gateway-postgres-dedup-reference-20261121`: an **unwired, separate OTP gateway** PostgreSQL ledger reference (`src/platform/ingress/otp-gateway-postgres-ledger.reference.ts` and `docs/reference/otp-gateway-idempotency-ledger.sql`) uses a durable unique HMAC idempotency-key reservation **committed before external delivery**. Under concurrency, only the first claimant receives `send`; identical pending duplicates receive `in_flight` (no resend), fingerprint mismatches receive `conflict` and accepted duplicates reuse a previously sanitized provider receipt. Provider acknowledgement uses a fingerprint-guarded `RESERVED` → `ACCEPTED` compare-and-set. The table stores only keyed identifiers, status, timestamps and an opaque provider reference—never an OTP code, phone, email or access token.
- Tests use a disposable isolated PostgreSQL schema when `DATABASE_URL` is configured and check concurrent claims, conflicting payload, persisted acceptance, fail-closed database errors, input validation and data-minimizing schema. **No automatic reservation expiry/reclaim or vendor send/retry is implemented**: an ambiguous provider dispatch must be reconciled before it can safely be retried. This code and SQL are NOT deployed, not a Prisma migration, not wired into the Nest app, and do not establish gateway atomic send/ack across external systems, approved provider, KZ residency, ingress or staging acceptance. No checkbox changes or 21/117 count increase. Full CI and merge pending.


### 2026-11-21 — OTP gateway idempotency payload consistency (PR-head CI pending)

- Branch `feat/otp-gateway-idempotency-payload-fingerprint-20261121`: the still-unwired HTTPS gateway adapter adds an `X-Services-OTP-Payload-Fingerprint` header: a domain-separated HMAC-SHA256 over the immutable `Idempotency-Key` and the exact outbound allowlisted JSON payload, using its separate 256-bit gateway idempotency secret. The same committed challenge and payload yield matching key/fingerprint; a changed OTP, recipient or other payload with the **same challenge ID** retains the same idempotency key but has a *different* fingerprint. The 6-digit OTP is never exposed in an unhashed digest. The existing per-challenge key, no-raw-UUID body, and default-off Nest wiring remain unchanged.
- Tests model a reference gateway accepting identical retries, returning one receipt, and rejecting conflicting payloads with HTTP 409; conflict responses fail closed. **This is not distributed exactly-once delivery**: the real provider gateway must implement durable atomic deduplication, compare fingerprints in constant time as appropriate, verify transport authenticity, avoid logging sensitive fields, and test concurrency/restarts, failed/late sends, key rotation and Kazakhstan residency in staging. ### 2026-11-21 — stable per-challenge OTP gateway idempotency contract (PR-head CI pending)

- Branch `feat/guest-otp-challenge-delivery-idempotency-20261121`: pass the **committed database challenge UUID** from all three `GuestOtpDispatchService` purposes through `OtpDeliveryService` to the still-unwired HTTPS gateway. The gateway now requires the UUID and a separate 32-byte idempotency signing secret; it HMACs a domain-separated normalized UUID into a 64-hex `Idempotency-Key` header. Repeated requests for the same committed challenge use the same opaque key; distinct challenges use different keys, and neither raw challenge UUID nor secret is included in the outbound JSON payload. A newly issued challenge intentionally has a new key and retains abuse quota. Backend legacy guest cancellation service fail-fast guard (2026-11-18): Prepared in replacement PR; original PR #253 had green push and PR CI but conflicted after #254 merged. The internal GuestCancellationService.cancel path now validates canonical request UUID, PostgreSQL int4 expectedVersion increment range, and canonical 32-byte READ_CANCEL bearer before leasing a DB transaction, matching the idempotent service's fail-fast boundary. Unit regressions verify zero SQL for malformed input and one transaction delegation for valid input. This does not expose an HTTP endpoint; CI, session authentication and staging acceptance remain open.

Backend guest cancellation ledger TTL live-time insert (2026-11-18): Prepared in this PR. PostgreSQL idempotency reservation now sets expires_at to clock_timestamp()+1 day instead of transaction-start now()+1 day, matching live-clock replay expiry. Regression holds a transaction open before cancellation and asserts full TTL from reservation time; CI pending, no authenticated HTTP/staging acceptance claimed.

Backend direct guest cancellation int4 boundary (2026-11-18): Prepared in this PR. The legacy internal cancelVerifiedGuestRequest transaction rejects expectedVersion outside 1..2147483646 before any SQL, matching idempotent/preflight cancellation, and regression checks invalid numeric boundaries with zero DB calls. CI pending; no HTTP BFF/staging acceptance claimed.

Backend guest contact expiry regressions (2026-11-18): Prepared in this PR. PostgreSQL fixtures test expiration of verified guest contact during an already open transaction before fresh cancellation and after completed idempotent cancellation; both must reject the expired contact by live database time and preserve request state/event/notification counts. CI pending; no authenticated HTTP or staging acceptance claimed.

Backend guest cancellation fresh proof/contact row-lock (2026-11-18): Prepared in this PR. Fresh guest cancellation rechecks READ_CANCEL capability joined to the same verified unexpired guest contact with PostgreSQL FOR SHARE OF c,g after request row lock, and uses contact channel from that locked row, preventing concurrent revocation between check and state write. PostgreSQL regression rejects revoked verification without mutating request. CI pending; authenticated HTTP and staging gates remain open.

Backend guest cancellation live capability expiry across PostgreSQL transaction waits (2026-11-17): Prepared in PR. All READ_CANCEL capability and verified guest contact expiry checks in fresh and replay transaction paths use PostgreSQL clock_timestamp() rather than BEGIN-frozen now(). PostgreSQL long-transaction regressions reject expired proofs before a fresh transition and on completed replay without duplicate cancellation event. CI pending; authenticated HTTP/staging acceptance remains open.

Backend guest cancellation PostgreSQL version bound (2026-11-17): Prepared in this PR. Cancellation expectedVersion is bounded to 1..2147483646 because service_requests.version is PostgreSQL signed int4, and successful cancellation increments it exactly once. Raw JSON, direct command and service input regressions reject int4 overflow before DB transaction; existing last valid version is preserved. CI, authenticated HTTP E2E and staging acceptance remain open.

Backend guest cancellation service pre-DB command and proof validation (2026-11-17): Prepared in this PR. GuestCancellationService.cancelIdempotently rejects malformed request IDs, unsafe expectedVersion, invalid idempotency headers and noncanonical 32-byte READ_CANCEL tokens before taking a pool connection; database transaction still revalidates proof and state. Unit regressions assert zero transactions on malformed submissions. No public endpoint, trusted BFF session or staging acceptance is added; CI pending.

Backend cancellation service preflight with live PostgreSQL (2026-11-17): Prepared in this PR. Real fixture verifies malformed Origin, CSRF and raw JSON are rejected before any transaction, valid preflight cancels once with a stable replay, one cancellation event and two notification intents. Caller-supplied transport in this test represents preverified BFF context; no HTTP route, actual session binding or staging acceptance is claimed. Branch/PR CI pending.

Backend cancellation service BFF preflight composition (2026-11-17): Prepared in this PR. Internal, unmounted GuestCancellationService.cancelWithPreflight composes strict transport/Origin/CSRF/raw JSON validation with transaction-backed idempotent cancellation. Unit regressions refuse malformed/cross-site requests before SQL and exercise the valid delegation path; requires trusted server session context, never browser-supplied sessionExpectedCsrf, expectedOrigin or requestProtocol. Authenticated HTTP E2E, frontend sealed capability, staging and operational acceptance remain open. Branch/PR CI pending.

Backend cancellation Nest service PostgreSQL delegation (2026-11-17): Prepared in this PR. Live DB fixture invokes GuestCancellationService.cancelIdempotently twice through caller-owned transaction adapter and asserts one cancellation event, two notice intents and a stable replay; a foreign READ_CANCEL capability is rejected. Branch/PR CI pending. This does not represent mounted/authenticated HTTP E2E or staging acceptance.

Backend guest cancellation idempotent Nest service wiring (2026-11-17): Prepared in this PR. Internal GuestCancellationService.cancelIdempotently invokes existing PostgreSQL atomic cancellation ledger within DatabasePool.transaction, fails closed without canonical distinct 256-bit guest capability pepper and independent ledger HMAC secret. Unit tests verify configuration rejection before DB and transaction invocation. No HTTP endpoint mounted; CI, provider delivery and staging acceptance remain open.

Backend guest cancellation ledger expiry fail-closed replay (2026-11-17): Prepared in PR. Completed ledger is valid only when expires_at is later than live PostgreSQL clock_timestamp(), not frozen transaction-start now(); PostgreSQL regression proves expired completed result cannot replay and no duplicate cancellation event appears. Branch/PR CI pending; no public HTTP route or release acceptance enabled.

Backend cancellation saved replay JSON shape (2026-11-17): Prepared in this PR. Only the exact {id,state,version} JSON object is accepted from a COMPLETED idempotency record; arrays, missing and extra fields are rejected. PostgreSQL replay regression added. Branch/PR CI pending; HTTP/staging acceptance remains open.

Backend guest cancellation replay final proof lock (2026-11-17): Prepared in this PR. After ledger and request locks, replay revalidates active READ_CANCEL guest capability and verified unexpired contact with PostgreSQL FOR SHARE row locks; test covers contact expiration and denies replay despite COMPLETED ledger. CI and staging acceptance pending. No public route enabled or release checkbox marked complete.

Backend guest cancellation exact replay version and state invariant (2026-11-17): Prepared in this PR. Previously committed replay responses must report exactly expectedVersion+1, and authoritative locked request status/version must match. PostgreSQL tests simulate forged higher response version alongside advanced request version, and changed request status at unchanged version. Branch/PR CI pending; unmounted HTTP contract and staging acceptance remain open.

PostgreSQL branch/PR CI and post-merge main evidence pending. No HTTP route enabled, and no broad release checkbox marked complete.

Receiver durable retry deadline foundation (2026-11-17): branch `backend/notification-receiver-durable-retry-20261117` adds PostgreSQL schedule table keyed by receiver ingress ID and current-lease-token-bound scheduling for 1s–1h delays. Production database operations, secrets and deployment remain pending.

The function is a **transaction core**, not an HTTP route, and requires a trusted authenticated customer identity from its caller.

### 2026-11-20 — registered customer completion bearer HTTP integration

- New bearer-only `POST /v1/account/requests/:id/complete` in the existing account-session module. Requires canonical 43-character bearer, strong quoted `If-Match` request version, and empty JSON object; customer ownership is resolved from an active/revocation-checked session and person row inside the same PostgreSQL transaction. Foreign and unknown requests produce the same 404, stale version is 409, malformed calls 400, missing/revoked bearer 401. No browser cookie authentication or user-selected customer ID is accepted.
- New fail-fast real Nest HTTP/PostgreSQL test covers foreign-account denial, bad preconditions/body, successful completion, no duplicate experience/outbox on replay, and revoked-session denial. The prior completion database transaction remains the authority for `ACCEPTED -> COMPLETED` and audit/notification writes.
- **Not complete:** same-origin browser BFF with CSRF, end-to-end frontend/admin browser flows, guest completion and policy review, staging deployment and security sign-off. No further top-level acceptance checkboxes are closed until those gates are met.

### 2026-11-20 — registered customer request inventory and pagination

- Introduces `GET /v1/account/requests` in the live account bearer-session module. Authenticated user -> customer person is resolved inside the same transaction as the bounded, latest-first 20-item PostgreSQL query. Returns only id/state/version/created_at and opaque keyset cursor; excludes guest contacts, notes, seller-private identifiers, proofs and other customers' requests. Unknown/expired/revoked bearer is denied.
- New CI-gated real HTTP/PostgreSQL test verifies scoped 22-item pagination, foreign-account isolation, no PII in response, malformed cursor rejection and immediate revoked-session denial. The response is `private, no-store`.
- This is a backend read primitive; frontend customer list/page, browser CSRF completion flow, staging and end-to-end request lifecycle remain OPEN. No top-level acceptance item is closed ahead of verified full CI and browser acceptance.

### 2026-11-20 — prepared separately-scoped guest completion authority

- Added `COMPLETE_REQUEST` PostgreSQL enum migration and purpose-isolated HMAC capability. Existing `READ_CANCEL` / `CREATE_REQUEST` / `MANAGE_SCHEDULE` tokens **cannot** be silently promoted. The prepared `confirmGuestRequestCompletion()` transaction requires a request-bound, unexpired, unconsumed completion proof linked to the exact guest contact; it locks request and proof, enforces `ACCEPTED` and exact version, atomically consumes one-use authority, records sanitized audit and outbox, notifies the seller once, and never creates registered-Person review eligibility for a guest.
- New real PostgreSQL CI-gated test covers success, duplicate proof denial, wrong purpose, mismatched contact, foreign request, expiry, stale version and pending-state denial. This is **prepared backend transaction core only**. No external HTTP route or new capability issuance/recovery flow exists; independently verified guest OTP/recovery, browser CSRF, rate limiting, staging and policy sign-off remain mandatory before exposure. **The 21/117 count does not change.**

### 2026-11-20 — guest completion independent OTP re-verification (backend internal)

- Added a separate `GuestChallengePurpose.COMPLETE_REQUEST` migration and backend-only issuance/dispatch/verify path. An active request-bound `READ_CANCEL` proof can **only initiate delivery of an OTP to the originally verified encrypted contact**; it cannot authorize completion. All existing global, network and recipient OTP quotas apply. Invalid/rejected provider delivery revokes the issued challenge. Correct OTP is attempt-limited and atomically issues a short-lived, request-bound, one-use `COMPLETE_REQUEST` capability; a stale challenge or wrong request cannot authorize completion.
- New PostgreSQL E2E gate `test/guest-completion-otp-db.test.ts` verifies dispatch and provider purpose, wrong code, atomic verification, consumed challenge, exact request binding, `ACCEPTED → COMPLETED` with outbox/notification and no guest review entitlement. No HTTP controller or browser cookie route is added here. Public enablement still requires separately reviewed network abuse boundaries, secure BFF CSRF, staging testing, real provider/OTP integration and owner approval. **Checklist remains 21/117.**

### 2026-11-20 — guest completion prepared service-to-service HTTP contract

- Added separately disabled (`SERVICES_ENABLE_GUEST_COMPLETION_HTTP==='enabled'`) BFF-only routes `POST /v1/guest-requests/:id/completion/challenges`, `POST /v1/guest-requests/:id/completion/challenges/:challengeId/verify`, and `POST /v1/guest-requests/:id/completion/confirm`. Requires trusted signed ingress network proof for OTP delivery, original request's READ_CANCEL capability to initiate OTP only, 6-digit code to mint a separately scoped COMPLETE_REQUEST proof, and a strong quoted If-Match + empty JSON body to perform a one-use, transactionally versioned confirmation. All route responses are no-store and reveal no private contact, OTP or DB details. The verified COMPLETE_REQUEST bearer in the verify response is intended **only for the trusted server-side BFF**, never browser JavaScript.
- Dedicated isolated real Nest HTTP regressions cover default-off zero effects, denied unsigned network requests, forged input, verification and confirmation preconditions and no-store response contract. Existing PostgreSQL tests for one-use scope and OTP remain required in CI.
- **Not a production activation:** real ingress blocklist/TLS, key custody, signed proof header stripping, recipient/network limits, approved OTP delivery provider, same-origin browser BFF with HttpOnly cookie + CSRF, end-to-end browser/staging and guest completion acceptance remain OPEN. Default remains disabled. **Checklist stays 21/117.**

### 2026-11-20 — shared OTP quota coverage for completion purpose

- Fixed a newly discovered **abuse-budget gap**: the contact-level send quota and shared global 1-minute/1-hour/1-day quota previously filtered only CREATE_REQUEST and MANAGE_SCHEDULE challenges, while completion issued COMPLETE_REQUEST. Both now count all three purposes together; the already request-bound network quota also includes COMPLETE_REQUEST.
- Existing real PostgreSQL guest completion OTP CI test now inserts challenge records with only the new purpose and checks that per-contact cooldown and global minute limit are enforced. These quotas remain required in the same transaction that commits a challenge, and default-off HTTP/provider signoffs remain OPEN. **No top-level checkbox change: 21/117.**
